❯ Create the spec-driven development scaffolding:                               
  1. specs/README.md: how specs work in this repo (statuses Draft → Approved →  
  Implemented, EARS requirements, AC IDs with a per-spec prefix, test names     
  must contain AC IDs, the spec skill and its template), plus an index table of 
  specs (number, name, prefix, status) that must be kept up to date.            
  2. scripts/traceability.ts and the npm script "trace" (run with tsx): collect 
  every AC defined as a heading "### <PFX>-ACnn" in specs//spec.md; collect AC  
  IDs mentioned in test names in test/ and src/ (.test.ts); print a table of    
  AC, spec and test files. An AC with "Level: ci" (its proof is a CI job, for   
  example Terraform validation) counts as covered only if it also has a         
  non-empty line "- Verified by: <command or CI job>". Exit with code 1 when an 
  AC of a spec with Status "Implemented" is not covered, or when a test         
  mentions an AC that no spec defines. ACs of Draft or Approved specs are       
  reported as pending and do not fail. It must work when specs/ has no specs    
  yet. With "npm run trace -- --write" it also writes the table to              
  docs/traceability.md.                                                         
  3. Unit tests for the parser, with fixture specs and fixture test names under 
  test/fixtures/. The script ignores test/fixtures/, and fixture AC IDs never   
  appear in real test titles.                                                   
     Run npm run check and npm run trace.

⏺ The spec scaffolding and the traceability check are in place. npm run check
  passes, and npm run trace exits 0 with an empty table, since there are no
  specs yet.

  What changed
  - specs/README.md: explains statuses, ID prefixes, EARS patterns, the exact AC
    heading format the checker reads, the ci + Verified by rule, the
    requirement that test names contain AC IDs, how to use npm run trace, and an
    empty index table.
  - scripts/traceability.ts and npm run trace: the checker. It prints the AC
    table and exits 1 on any of these:
    - an AC of an Implemented spec has no proof;
    - a test names an AC that no spec defines;
    - a spec is malformed.

    --write also writes docs/traceability.md. That file is generated, so I added
    it to .prettierignore.
  - Fixtures in test/fixtures/traceability/: three small sample repos (passing,
    failing, invalid) and a file of sample test names. Every fixture ID starts
    with ZZ, and a unit test checks that no real test name contains one.
  - test/unit/traceability.test.ts: 13 tests.

  What was verified
  - npm run check: typecheck, lint, format and 24 unit tests all pass.
  - Running the script directly on the fixtures gives exit 0 for passing, 1 for
    failing and invalid, and 2 for an unknown argument.

  Decisions I made that your brief didn't cover (easy to undo):
  - A spec with a missing or unknown status, or an AC ID defined twice, fails
    the check even if the spec is a draft. Otherwise the checker can't tell
    whether the spec is Implemented, or which spec an ID belongs to.
  - Tests marked .skip or .todo don't count as proof, but they are still checked
    for undefined IDs.
  - ### …-ACnn headings inside code fences are ignored.
  - Known limits: a describe.skip doesn't mark the tests inside it as skipped,
    and it.each(...) titles aren't detected.

  What is left
  - CI doesn't run npm run trace yet; only /ship does. Adding it to
    .github/workflows/ci.yml is a one-line change if you want it.
  - I haven't generated docs/traceability.md. That's worth doing once there are
    specs.
  - Next in this phase: specs/000-overview/spec.md with the currency table, then
    the module specs.

  Nothing is committed.

  1. Detect the titles of it.each, test.each and describe.each, including their

      67 -`npm run trace` looks for AC IDs in the titles of `describe`, `
         -suite`, `it` and `test` calls in every `*.test.ts` file under `
         -src/` and `test/`. IDs in comments, assertions or other strings

         -fixtures/` is ignored: it holds sample specs and tests for the 
         -checker itself.                                                
      67 +`npm run trace` parses every `*.test.ts` file under `src/` and 
         +`test/` and reads the titles of `describe`, `suite`, `it` and `
         +test` calls, including `.each` and `.for` (table-driven tests, 
         +with any of `.skip`, `.only`, `.todo` and `.concurrent`), `.ski

      68 +                                                               
      69 +- A test's name is its `describe` titles plus its own title, so

⏺ Both limits are fixed, and CI now runs npm run trace right after npm run 
  check. npm run check passes (27 unit tests), and npm run trace exits 0 with no
  specs yet. Run directly on the sample repos, the checker exits 0 for passing
  and 1 for failing and invalid.

  What changed
  - scripts/traceability.ts: it now finds test names by parsing each test file
    with the TypeScript parser instead of a regex. A regex couldn't follow which
    tests sit inside which describe.
    - Table-driven tests: it reads the titles of it, test and describe with
      .each and .for, in their skip, only, todo and concurrent forms.
      Template-table .each and conditional .skipIf / .runIf work too. An AC ID
      in the title of an it.each(cases)('MOV-AC07 … %s') counts as covered, so
      spec 006's test will work. An ID that appears only in the table does not
      count.
    - Nesting: a test's name is its describe titles plus its own title. Tests
      inside a describe.skip or describe.todo don't count as proof. A describe
      with no running test inside doesn't count either, but an ID in it that no
      spec defines still fails the check.
  - Fixtures: two new samples, each.test.ts (all the forms above) and
    nesting.test.ts. The passing sample repo now has an AC covered through its
    describe title and one covered by a table-driven test. The failing one has
    an AC whose only test is inside a skipped describe.
  - Unit tests: 4 new or updated tests, 17 in this file. They check the exact
    list of names and covered IDs.
  - CI: - run: npm run trace added after npm run check in
    .github/workflows/ci.yml.
  - Docs: specs/README.md now describes the naming rules, and the fixtures
    README lists the new files. In docs/dependencies.md, the typescript row now
    says its parser is also used by the checker. That isn't a new dependency,
    but Prettier re-aligned the whole table.

  Limits left
  - Tests registered through test.extend(...) aren't detected.
  - Titles that aren't written as text in the call (a variable or a function
    name) are read as empty.

  Nothing is committed.

❯ Using the spec skill, write specs/000-overview/spec.md with ID prefix SYS.    
  This spec gives the shared context every other spec relies on.                
  Include:                                                                      
  - Goal: a balance service for a FinTech, where customer money must never be   
  lost, duplicated or exposed.                                                  
  - Actors and roles: customer (owns accounts; reads them; withdraws from and   
  transfers out of own accounts), operator (simulates payment rails with        
  deposits; reverses transactions; freezes, unfreezes and closes accounts), and 
  the system (internal settlement accounts).                                    
  - Glossary: account (customer or system), currency and minor units, amount,   
  transaction, ledger entry, balance (cached for customer accounts, derived for 
  system accounts), idempotency key, reversal, problem details.                 
  - Global invariants, each as an AC proven by tests in later specs: entries of 
  every transaction sum to zero per currency; customer balances are never       
  negative; the sum of all balances per currency, system accounts included, is  
  zero; every customer cached balance equals the sum of its entries; ledger     
  entries are never updated or deleted.                                         
  - Non-functional requirements: stateless replicas behind a load balancer;     
  correctness under concurrency before throughput; p99 latency under 300 ms for 
  single movements at 200 requests per second on a laptop (measured and         
  reported, not promised); every request has a correlation id; every money      
  movement has an audit record.                                                 
  - Supported currencies and their ISO 4217 exponents: USD 2, MXN 2, EUR 2 and  
  COP 2 for our assumed launch markets, plus JPY 0, which has no minor units    
  and keeps that case covered by tests.                                         
  - Out of scope: FX between currencies, interest and fees, statements,         
  KYC/AML, multi-region, real payment rails.                                    
    Do not write code.

       40 -| **Account**            | A container of money in a single cu
          -rrency, fixed when it is opened (Q4). Either a customer accoun
          -t or a system account.                                        
          -                                                              
          -                                                              
          -                  |                                           
       41 -| **Customer account**   | An account owned by exactly one cus
          -tomer. It has a status (active, frozen or closed) and a cached
          - balance. The effect of each status on movements is defined in
          - the accounts spec.                                           
          -                                                              
          -                 |                                            
       42 -| **System account**     | An internal account owned by the se
          -rvice, for example the settlement account of a currency (Q12).
          - It has no owner, no cached balance and may go below zero. No 
          -movement locks or updates its row.                            
          -                                                              
          -                 |                                            
       43 -| **Currency**           | An ISO 4217 alphabetic code from th
          -e table in 1.3.                                               
          -                                                              
          -                                                              
          -                                                              
          -                   |                                          
       44 -| **Minor units**        | The smallest unit of a currency. A 
          -currency's exponent is the number of minor units in one major 
          -unit as a power of ten: exponent 2 means 100 cents per dollar,
          - exponent 0 means the major unit is the minor unit.           
          -                                                              
          -                   |                                          
       45 -| **Amount**             | An integer number of minor units to
          -gether with a currency. `bigint` in the domain; in the API a s
          -tring of decimal digits plus a currency code, for example `{"a
          -mount": "1050", "currency": "EUR"}` for 10.50 EUR and `{"amoun
          -t": "1050", "currency": "JPY"}` for 1050 yen. Never a float or
          - JSON number. |                                               
       46 -| **Money movement**     | A deposit, withdrawal, transfer or 
          -reversal. Each one is recorded as exactly one transaction.    
          -                                                              
          -                                                              
          -

⏺ I wrote specs/000-overview/spec.md (ID prefix SYS, status Draft) and added it
  to the index in specs/README.md. npm run check passes, and npm run trace lists
  all 20 SYS ACs as pending, as expected for a Draft spec. No code was written.

  What the spec contains
  - Section 1: the goal (never lost, duplicated or exposed), a table of
    operations each role may perform, the glossary, and the currency table (USD,
    MXN, EUR and COP with exponent 2, JPY with 0).
  - 25 requirements, 20 acceptance criteria:

  ┌──────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────┐
  │   ACs    │              What they cover              │      Level       │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │          │ Who may do what, missing credentials, and │                  │
  │ AC01–03  │  the same 404 for foreign, unknown and    │ integration      │
  │          │ system accounts                           │                  │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ AC04–07  │ Currencies, amount format and the JPY     │ unit and         │
  │          │ case                                      │ integration      │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │          │ Your five invariants, each with concrete  │                  │
  │ AC08,    │ numbers, plus a check that the database   │ integration      │
  │ AC10–13  │ itself rejects direct writes that break   │                  │
  │          │ them                                      │                  │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ AC09     │ A movement that fails halfway leaves      │ integration      │
  │          │ nothing behind                            │                  │
  ├──────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────┤
  │          │ Non-functional: stateless replicas,       │ AC14 and AC17    │
  │ AC14–20  │ crossed concurrent transfers, retry       │ e2e, the rest    │
  │          │ bounds, the load-test report, correlation │ integration      │
  │          │  id, audit record, problem details        │                  │
  └──────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────┘

  - Latency: AC17 reports a p99 at or above 300 ms as a missed target. It
    doesn't fail the test, as you asked.
  - Added beyond your brief: AC09 for atomicity, and AC01–AC07 plus AC16 and
    AC20 for rules every spec shares (auth and roles, amount and currency
    validation, retries, error format). They come from AGENTS.md and the
    planning decisions.
  - Error catalogue: the errors every spec shares (401, 403, 404, 400,
    503, 500). Each capability spec will add its own.

  14 open questions, each with a recommended answer. Requirements that depend on
  one cite it and follow my recommendation until you decide. These shape later
  specs the most:
  - Q1: customers open their own accounts.
  - Q2: operators can read any customer account.
  - Q4: one currency per account.
  - Q5: amounts from 1 to 9223372036854775807.
  - Q6: an operation the caller's role isn't allowed answers 403, not 404.
  - Q8: 5 attempts, then 503.
  - Q9: the correlation id goes in X-Request-Id, and the client's value is kept
    only if it passes a safe pattern.
  - Q10: a separate append-only audit table; freeze, unfreeze and close get
    audit records too.
  - Q11: ledger entries carry signed amounts.
  - Q12: one settlement account per currency.

  The rest are smaller details: one role per user, what a customer read returns,
  whether a 404 is stored for replay, and how the latency test is run.

  Conflicts: none, since this is the first spec. Decisions that later specs
  (accounts, idempotency, movements) must pick up are marked in the text.

  Approving the spec accepts the recommended answers unless you change them.

※ recap: We're in phase 02-specs, writing the specs before any code; the traceability check is done and the overview spec 000 is drafted with 14 open questions. Next, you review those questions and approve the spec or change the recommended answers. (disable recaps in /config)

❯ Changes to spec 000 before we continue:                                       
  1. Status codes for bad input. 400 only when the request itself is malformed: 
  JSON that does not parse, a missing or malformed required header such as      
  Idempotency-Key, or an invalid pagination cursor. A request that parses but   
  whose content fails validation (amount format or range, unknown currency,     
  missing or unknown fields) answers 422 with type /problems/validation-error   
  and an errors member listing each failing field. The capability specs use 422 
  for their business rejections too, so clients get one rule: 400 means the     
  request is broken, 422 means it was understood and refused. Update every      
  requirement, AC and catalogue row that says 400.                              
  2. Q5, decided: 1 to 9223372036854775807 stays as the range every amount must 
  fit. State that the ledger spec adds a configurable maximum per movement,     
  default "100000000000", also answered with 422.                               
  3. Q8, decided: 3 attempts in total, not 5, with the same backoff, then 503   
  with Retry-After: 1. With ordered locks a deadlock should not happen at all,  
  so the retry is only a safety net, and more attempts just stretch latency     
  under load.                                                                   
  4. SYS-R01: /metrics also needs no credentials, because the load balancer     
  keeps it private instead (spec 007).                                          
     Mark Q5 and Q8 as decided by the owner. The other questions stay open      
  until I review all specs together.

⏺ Spec 000 is updated with all four changes. It now has 28 requirements and 21
  ACs, still Draft. npm run check passes, and npm run trace lists all 21 ACs as
  pending.

  What changed
  1. 400 vs 422
     - Section 1 now states the rule: 400 means the request is broken, 422 means
       it was understood and refused.
     - SYS-R07 (amount) and SYS-R09 (currency) now answer 422.
     - Three new requirements:
       - SYS-R26: bad JSON, a missing or malformed required header such as
         Idempotency-Key, or an invalid cursor answers 400.
       - SYS-R27: content that fails validation answers 422 with one errors
         entry per failing field.
       - SYS-R28: business rejections answer 422, with a problem type set by
         each capability spec.
     - SYS-AC05, AC07 and AC20 now expect 422 and an errors member. SYS-AC10's 7
       rejected withdrawals now answer 422, which proves SYS-R28.
     - New SYS-AC21 checks the boundary: three broken withdrawals and a bad
       cursor answer 400; an unknown field and a body with two failing fields
       answer 422.
     - The error catalogue splits the old 400 row into 400 (malformed), 422
       (validation) and 422 (business rule).
  2. Q5 decided: every amount must fit 1 to 9223372036854775807. The glossary
     and Q5 both say the ledger spec adds a configurable maximum per movement,
     default "100000000000", also answered with 422.
  3. Q8 decided: 3 attempts in total, then 503 with Retry-After: 1. Q8 records
     your safety-net reasoning. SYS-R18, SYS-R19, SYS-AC16 and the catalogue row
     are updated.
  4. /metrics: SYS-R01 lets it through without credentials, kept private by the
     load balancer (spec 007), and SYS-AC02 checks that it answers 200.

  Two new open questions, raised by your 400/422 change:
  - Q15: the shape of an errors entry. I recommend a JSON Pointer to the field
    plus a detail message.
  - Q16: the problem type for 400. I used /problems/malformed-request, since
    your brief only named the 422 type.

  Q1–Q4, Q6, Q7 and Q9–Q14 stay open for your joint review of all specs. There
  are no other specs yet, so nothing conflicts.

✻ Churned for 1m 27s · done 4:41 PM

❯ Using the spec skill, write specs/001-accounts/spec.md with ID prefix ACC.    
  Capability: customer accounts and their lifecycle.                            
  Acceptance criteria must cover at least:                                      
  - A customer creates an account in a supported currency; it starts active     
  with balance "0". A customer may hold several accounts, including several in  
  the same currency. Account creation accepts an optional Idempotency-Key with  
  the semantics of spec 005.                                                    
  - Unsupported or malformed currency codes are rejected with 422.              
  - A customer reads one of their own accounts (id, currency, status, balance   
  as a digit string in minor units, timestamps) and lists their accounts with   
  cursor pagination.                                                            
  - Reading or listing another customer's account answers 404, never 403, so    
  account existence is not revealed.                                            
  - An operator can read any customer account.                                  
  - Status lifecycle: active → frozen → active, and active or frozen → closed.  
  Closed is final.                                                              
  - Frozen and closed accounts cannot send or receive customer movements; they  
  can still be read.                                                            
  - Closing an account with a non-zero balance is rejected with 409.            
  - Only operators can freeze, unfreeze or close.                               
  - Account history: a customer lists the ledger entries of their own account,  
  newest first, with cursor pagination on (created_at, id); the cursor is       
  opaque, and a tampered cursor is rejected with 400.                           
  - System accounts are never visible through customer endpoints.               
    Do not write code.

      102 +| 001 | [accounts](001-accounts/spec.md) | ACC    | Draft  |  

⏺ I wrote specs/001-accounts/spec.md (ID prefix ACC, status Draft) and added it
  to the index. It has 26 requirements and 23 acceptance criteria. npm run check
  passes, and npm run trace lists 44 pending ACs across the two specs, none
  failing. No code was written.

  What it covers. Every point in your brief has at least one AC:
  - Creating accounts (AC01–06): several accounts per customer, including in the
    same currency; the optional Idempotency-Key, scoped per user, with spec
    005's semantics; 422 for a bad currency; 403 when an operator tries.
  - Reading (AC07–10, AC18, AC22): the account fields, digit-string balances, a
    JPY account, the paged account list and the newest-first history. Another
    customer's account answers 404 exactly like an unknown id, never 403. An
    operator can read any account. System accounts never appear.
  - Lifecycle (AC11–17, AC23): the allowed transitions, closed as final, 409
    when closing an account with money, operators only, and frozen and closed
    accounts unable to send or receive but still readable. AC14 checks that a
    status change and a movement racing on the same account always end up one
    after the other: a closed account never ends up holding money.
  - Cursors (AC19–21): new entries arriving while a client pages cause no
    repeats or gaps; entries at the same instant are ordered by id; an altered
    or foreign cursor answers 400, and a bad limit answers 422.

  Conflicts with spec 000. I haven't edited spec 000, since these change its
  content:
  1. 409 vs 422. Spec 000 says capability specs use 422 for business rejections,
     but your brief wants 409 for closing an account with money. My
     recommendation (Q6): 409 only when a status change conflicts with the
     account's current state, 422 for everything else, and spec 000 names this
     exception.
  2. Questions your brief answers. It settles spec 000's Q1 (customers create
     accounts), Q2 (operators read any account) and Q14 (history is the
     account's ledger entries). Spec 000's role table still says "Pending" for
     those.

  Tell me whether to update spec 000 for both.

  Open questions that matter most (14 in total):
  - Q5: asking for the status an account already has answers 200 and changes
    nothing, so an operator can retry safely. Freezing or unfreezing a closed
    account answers 409.
  - Q7: a reversal is allowed on a frozen account but not on a closed one.
  - Q8: a transfer to another customer's frozen or closed account answers
    exactly like a transfer to an account that doesn't exist, so the sender
    learns nothing.
  - Q4: cursors are signed with a new CURSOR_SECRET setting, shared by all
    replicas. That needs npm run env:sync and an ADR in phase 03.
  - Q14: to keep paging gap-free, an entry's timestamp is taken after the
    account lock is held, not when its database transaction starts. Otherwise a
    slow transaction could slip an older entry behind a page the client already
    read. This one goes to the ledger spec and an ADR.

  The others: no operator account creation (Q1), no limit on accounts per
  customer (Q2), page size 20 by default and 100 at most (Q3), what a history
  entry shows (Q9), what operators can read (Q10), operators can't reach system
  accounts (Q11), a customer is the token subject (Q12), and timestamp format
  (Q13).

  Operation paths are proposed in section 1.1 and become final in the OpenAPI
  document in phase 08.

✻ Worked for 4m 11s · done 4:46 PM

※ recap: We're writing phase 02 specs for the balance service; specs 000 overview and 001 accounts are drafted, and check and trace pass. Next, tell me whether to update spec 000 for the 409 exception and the questions spec 001 answers. (disable recaps in /config)

❯ Decisions for specs 000 and 001:                                              
  1. Q6 of spec 001, decided, as one rule for every spec: 409 means the request 
  conflicts with the current state of the resource it acts on (closing an       
  account that holds money, changing the status of a closed account, and later  
  reversing a transaction already reversed or reusing an idempotency key still  
  in progress). 422 means a money movement refused by a business rule,          
  including a movement on a frozen or closed account. Write this rule in spec   
  000 next to the 400/422 rule. Spec 001 already follows it.                    
  2. Spec 000: my brief for spec 001 settles its Q1 (customers open their own   
  accounts), Q2 (operators read any customer account) and Q14 (the history is   
  the account's ledger entries). Mark them decided by the owner and update the  
  role table.                                                                   
  3. Q4 of spec 001, decided: cursors signed with HMAC-SHA256 and               
  CURSOR_SECRET, a secret separate from JWT_SECRET so that one key never serves 
  two purposes, shared by all replicas.                                         
     The other open questions of spec 001 stay open until I review all specs    
  together.

⏺ All three decisions are applied to specs 000 and 001. npm run check passes,
  and npm run trace lists 45 ACs across the two specs, all pending and none
  failing.

  Spec 000 (now 29 requirements, 22 ACs)
  - Status-code rule: section 1 now lists 400, 409 and 422 side by side, using
    your wording and examples.
    - SYS-R28 now covers only money movements refused by a business rule (422).
    - New SYS-R29: a conflict with the current state of the resource
      answers 409.
    - New SYS-AC22 proves SYS-R29 with spec 001's two conflicts: closing an
      account that holds "1" EUR, and freezing a closed account.
    - The error catalogue has a 422 row for movements refused by a business rule
      and a new 409 row.
  - Q1, Q2 and Q14 are marked decided by the owner. In the role table:
    - customers open their own accounts;
    - operators read any customer account;
    - a read returns the account's details, balance and ledger entries.

    Whether an operator may also open accounts stays pending as spec 001 Q1.
  - SYS-AC01 now includes the operator read, since that row of the role table is
    decided.

  Spec 001
  - Q6 decided: it points to the rule in spec 000. ACC-R14 no longer cites it.
  - Q4 decided: cursors are signed with HMAC-SHA256 using CURSOR_SECRET, kept
    separate from JWT_SECRET and shared by all replicas. ACC-R23 no longer cites
    it.
  - Q1 now says that spec 000 leaves the operator question to this spec.

  The other open questions of spec 001 stay pending for your joint review.

  Left for later
  - CURSOR_SECRET isn't in .env.example yet. It goes in when cursors are
    implemented in phase 08, followed by npm run env:sync, which generates a
    random value because the name ends in _SECRET.
  - The cursor design also needs its ADR in phase 03.

  Nothing is committed.

✻ Crunched for 1m 36s · done 4:52 PM

❯ /audit                                                                        
  ⎿  I found no Critical or High issues. There are 7 Medium findings and several Low ones, mostly in the new npm run trace checker and in testability gaps in the two draft specs.

     I reviewed everything on phase/02-specs against main (no commits yet, so all of it is uncommitted). That covers the 4 modified files and the untracked scripts/traceability.ts, test/unit/traceability.test.ts, test/fixtures/traceability/**, specs/README.md, specs/000-overview/spec.md and specs/001-accounts/spec.md. Typecheck, ESLint and Prettier pass on these files. The current repo passes npm run trace: no real test name contains an AC ID yet. I did not run Vitest.

     Medium

     1. scripts/traceability.ts:11 and :260-274: the checker scans files that Vitest never runs
     - What is wrong: the checker reads every *.test.ts under src/ and test/. vitest.config.ts:18-33 only runs test/unit/**, src/** and test/integration/**, and there is no e2e project.
     - How to trigger: add test/e2e/replicas.test.ts with it('SYS-AC14 …'). The trace marks SYS-AC14 as covered, but neither npm run check nor npm run test:integration runs that file, in CI or locally.
     - Impact: e2e ACs (SYS-AC14, SYS-AC17) can pass the gate without ever being run.
     - Suggested fix: take the file list from the Vitest project include globs, or fail on any *.test.ts that no project matches.
     - AC: missing AC (definition of done: "every touched AC has a passing test").

     2. scripts/traceability.ts:26-37 and :233-236: tests Vitest skips or inverts still count as proof
     - What is wrong: three forms that never prove the AC still mark it covered:
       - A test with no handler. The installed Vitest makes it todo (node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/run.BTlFXlnv.js:2669; suites at :2807).
       - An options argument such as { skip: true }, { todo: true } or { fails: true } (:2661). The checker never reads the second argument.
       - .fails. It is in MODIFIERS but not in NOT_RUN_MODIFIERS, and it passes only when the body fails.
     - How to trigger: each of these covers MOV-AC03:
       - it('MOV-AC03 later')
       - it('MOV-AC03 x', { skip: true }, fn)
       - describe('MOV-AC03', { todo: true }, () => { it('a', fn) })
       - it.fails('MOV-AC03 rejects …', fn)
     - Impact: an Implemented spec passes with no test that actually runs.
     - Suggested fix:
       - Treat a call without a function argument as todo.
       - Read skip, todo and fails from an object-literal argument, with child tests inheriting them from their suite.
       - Add fails to the not-run set.
       - Add fixtures for each case.
     - AC: missing AC.

     3. scripts/traceability.ts:15 and :115-121: a mistyped AC heading drops the AC silently
     - What is wrong: only ### PFX-ACnn followed by whitespace is recognised. Anything else is not parsed and no problem is reported.
     - How to trigger: ### ACC-AC05: Unsupported currencies, #### ACC-AC05 · … or ### ACC-AC5 · ….
     - Impact: the AC is not in the report at all, so it can never show as "missing", even once the spec is Implemented.
     - Suggested fix: report any line matching /^#{1,6}\s*[A-Z][A-Z0-9]*-AC\d/ that does not match AC_HEADING exactly. While there, check that each AC ID uses the spec's declared ID prefix.
     - AC: missing AC.

     4. scripts/traceability.ts:308-313: the gate cannot fail on a missing test before phase 13
     - What is wrong: an uncovered AC fails only in an Implemented spec. specs/README.md:24 and AGENTS.md:21 keep every spec Draft or Approved until 13-final-review.
     - How to trigger: implement ACC-AC13 in phase 06 with no test. CI and /ship stay green for seven phases.
     - Impact:
       - The rule in AGENTS.md:22 ("confirm every AC you implemented has a test") is not enforced.
       - The npm run trace description in AGENTS.md:79 ("Checks that every AC in specs/ has a test") overstates what it does.
     - Suggested fix: mark coverage per AC. For example, any AC listed as done in tasks.md, or with an - **Implemented in:** line, must be covered whatever the spec status. Then correct the AGENTS.md wording.
     - AC: missing AC.

     5. specs/000-overview/spec.md:239 vs :337: SYS-AC16 asks for something full jitter cannot guarantee
     - What is wrong: SYS-AC16 requires an "increasing, jittered and capped delay between attempts". Q8 fixes "full jitter", where each delay is random between 0 and the current bound, so the second delay is often shorter than the first.
     - How to trigger: any implementation that follows Q8, tested with a real random source.
     - Impact: the test is either flaky or forces a backoff that contradicts Q8.
     - Suggested fix: require the bound to increase (10, 20 … capped at 200 ms), each delay to lie within its bound, and the random source and sleep to be injected.
     - AC: SYS-AC16, SYS-R18.

     6. specs/001-accounts/spec.md:161, :193, :169 and :257: ACs depend on timestamps being strictly later or distinct
     - What is wrong:
       - ACC-AC07 and ACC-AC11 require updatedAt to be strictly later after each change.
       - ACC-AC08 and ACC-AC19 expect creation order to be kept by (createdAt, id).
       - Q13 fixes millisecond precision, and Fastify inject requests often finish within 1 ms.
     - How to trigger: freeze and then unfreeze in under 1 ms. Both get the same updatedAt, or the clock goes backwards across replicas or after an NTP step.
     - Impact: flaky integration tests, or pressure to add sleeps.
     - Suggested fix: use "not earlier than" (or a version counter) for updatedAt. For ordering, specify ids that increase monotonically, such as UUIDv7 with a monotonic counter, or assert order through the tie-break rule.
     - AC: ACC-AC07, ACC-AC08, ACC-AC11, ACC-AC19.

     7. specs/000-overview/spec.md:96 and :103: lock and statement timeouts end up as 500
     - What is wrong: SYS-R18 and SYS-R19 cover only 40P01 and 40001. Every other failure falls under SYS-R25 and answers 500.
     - How to trigger: a withdrawal waiting on a locked account hits lock_timeout (55P03) or statement_timeout (57014).
     - Impact: retryable overload gets a 500 instead of a 503 with Retry-After. The spec also says nothing about:
       - timeouts ordered database < service < load balancer;
       - keeping the idempotency wait timeout (409) separate from the lock timeout (503);
       - pool sizing and graceful shutdown.

       The shared error catalogue (:293-303) also has no 429 for rate limiting.
     - Suggested fix: add requirements and ACs to spec 000, or to the runtime spec 007 it already mentions, for the lock timeout (503), the ordered timeouts and 429.
     - AC: missing AC.

     Low

     8. scripts/traceability.ts:20 and :98-100: the Level line is not validated
     - What is wrong: Level values are never checked, and level ci counts as covered with any non-empty Verified by text.
     - How to trigger: - **Level:** integraton is silently treated as needing a test. - **Level:** ci with - **Verified by:** n/a passes the gate.
     - Impact: typos go unnoticed, and the test requirement can be bypassed.
     - Suggested fix: accept only unit, integration, e2e and ci, and report a Verified by line on any AC that is not level ci.
     - AC: missing AC.

     9. scripts/traceability.ts:18 and :109-112: code-fence detection is too simple
     - What is wrong: any line starting with three backticks or tildes toggles the fence, whatever fence opened the block.
     - How to trigger: a ```` block that contains ~~~, or a fence of four backticks around an example with a nested three-backtick fence.
     - Impact: real ACs after the block are dropped, or example headings are read as ACs.
     - Suggested fix: remember the opening fence's character and length, and close only on a matching fence.
     - AC: missing AC.

     10. test/unit/traceability.test.ts: documented behaviours have no tests
     - What is wrong: behaviours promised in specs/README.md or in code comments are never exercised:
       - "An undefined ID fails even in a skipped test" (specs/README.md:72). The fixture ZZE-AC01 sits in a running test.
       - An unknown ID in a suite title.
       - .skipIf and .runIf on tests counting as proof.
       - An empty .each table counting as proof.
     - How to trigger: a regression in any of these still passes the suite.
     - Impact: the checker's rules can change without any test noticing.
     - Suggested fix: add a fixture case for each.
     - AC: missing AC.

     11. specs/000-overview/spec.md:33: operators can deposit into "Any account"
     - What is wrong: the role table allows operators to deposit into "Any account", which includes system accounts. ACC Q11 (specs/001-accounts/spec.md:335) says system accounts answer 404 to operators.
     - How to trigger: an operator deposit into the EUR settlement account.
     - Impact: an implementer cannot tell whether this is allowed.
     - Suggested fix: change the cell to "Any customer account".
     - AC: SYS-R03.

     12. specs/000-overview/spec.md:84 vs specs/001-accounts/spec.md:68: signed history amounts
     - What is wrong: SYS-R06 says every API amount is a decimal-digit string. History entries carry signed strings such as "-1200".
     - How to trigger: reading any account history.
     - Impact: a strict reading of SYS-R06 contradicts section 1.4 of spec 001.
     - Suggested fix: allow a leading - on output amounts only, in SYS-R06.
     - AC: SYS-R06, ACC-R21.

     13. specs/001-accounts/spec.md:6, :129, :233 and :241: references to specs that do not exist
     - What is wrong: the spec depends on spec 005, the movements spec and the ledger spec, none of which exist yet.
     - How to trigger: trying to write tests for ACC-AC03, ACC-AC16 and ACC-AC17.
     - Impact: their expected answers ("as spec 005 defines", "the type the movements spec sets") cannot be checked yet. Neither can the audit's idempotency rules (key row first, stored rejections, 409/422), because spec 005 does not exist.
     - Suggested fix: write specs 002 to 007 before approving spec 001, then fill in the concrete problem types.
     - AC: ACC-AC03, ACC-AC16, ACC-AC17.

     14. specs/001-accounts/spec.md:328 vs :98: the cursor cannot tell two users' lists apart
     - What is wrong: the cursor payload in Q4 has no user id, but ACC-R23 requires a cursor "issued for a different list" to answer 400.
     - How to trigger: C2 uses C1's next cursor for GET /accounts.
     - Impact: it is accepted, not 400. Nothing leaks because the query filters by owner, but the spec cannot be met as written.
     - Suggested fix: add the user id to the payload, or narrow ACC-R23.
     - AC: ACC-R23.

     15. Missing ACs
     - What is wrong: three behaviours have no AC:
       - an operator's GET /accounts answering 403 (ACC Q10, specs/001-accounts/spec.md:334);
       - a JWT with alg: none or the wrong algorithm being rejected (SYS-AC02);
       - a deposit that would overflow the bigint cached balance.
     - How to trigger: each case today falls to an unspecified answer, or to 500 for the overflow.
     - Impact: these cases can be built and shipped untested.
     - Suggested fix: add an AC for each.
     - AC: missing AC.

     16. specs/000-overview/spec.md:79: /metrics has no authentication
     - What is wrong: /metrics needs no credentials, and only the load balancer keeps it private.
     - How to trigger: anyone who can reach a replica directly can read it.
     - Impact: operational metrics are exposed.
     - Suggested fix: serve metrics on a separate internal port, or require a token.
     - AC: SYS-R01, SYS-AC02.

     17. .prettierignore:9: the generated docs/traceability.md is never checked
     - What is wrong: the file is neither gitignored nor checked for freshness.
     - How to trigger: run npm run trace -- --write once. /ship stages everything (git add -A) and commits the file, and it goes stale after the next spec change.
     - Impact: the committed document drifts from the specs and tests.
     - Suggested fix: either gitignore it, or have CI regenerate it and fail if it changed.
     - AC: missing AC.

     Categories without findings

     - Money: no amount code in this change. The specs require bigint in the domain, digit strings in the API and the exponent from the currency table.
     - Transactions and concurrency: no code. The specs match AGENTS.md: ordered FOR UPDATE on customer accounts, system accounts never locked, checks after locks, bounded retries.
     - Idempotency: no code, and spec 005 does not exist yet (see 13).

     Verdict: fix before shipping

❯ Triage of the audit. Fix now, each change with a test where it applies.       
  Traceability checker (scripts/traceability.ts), with a fixture per case:      
  1. Only test files that a Vitest project runs count. Fail on any *.test.ts    
  that no project includes.                                                     
  2. A test or suite with no function argument is todo. skip, todo or fails in  
  an options object means not run, and suites pass it to their children. .fails 
  is not proof.                                                                 
  3. Report any heading that looks like an AC but does not match the exact      
  format, and any AC ID whose prefix is not the spec's declared prefix.         
  4. Accept only unit, integration, e2e and ci as Level, and report a Verified  
  by line on an AC that is not level ci.                                        
  5. Close a code fence only with a fence of the same character and at least    
  the same length.                                                              
  6. Add fixture tests for the documented behaviours that have none: an         
  undefined ID inside a skipped test, an unknown ID in a suite title, skipIf    
  and runIf, and an empty each table.                                           
  7. Enforce coverage before the final phase: an AC referenced by a ticked task 
  ("- [x]") in specs/<spec>/tasks.md must be covered whatever the spec status.  
  Add to AGENTS.md that a task is ticked in tasks.md when it is done, and make  
  the description of npm run trace there say exactly what it checks.            
     Specs:                                                                     
  8. SYS-AC16: the backoff bound grows (10, 20, 40 ms, capped at 200 ms), each  
  delay falls within its bound, and the random source and the sleep are         
  injected, so the test is deterministic.                                       
  9. ACC-AC07 and ACC-AC11: updatedAt is not earlier than before the change.    
  Ids are UUIDv7 generated monotonically, and ordering ACs assert the           
  (createdAt, id) rule instead of relying on distinct timestamps.               
  10. Spec 000: operators deposit into "any customer account", never a system   
  account. SYS-R06 allows a leading minus sign on amounts the API returns, such 
  as history entries, never on amounts it accepts.                              
  11. Spec 001: the cursor payload includes the user id, so a cursor from       
  another user's list answers 400. Add an AC for an operator listing accounts,  
  which answers 403.                                                            
  12. /metrics is served on a separate internal port, METRICS_PORT, that the    
  load balancer never routes to, still without credentials. Update SYS-R01 and  
  SYS-AC02.                                                                     
      Leave for later, no change now: lock and statement timeouts, ordered      
  timeouts, pool exhaustion and 429 belong to specs 003 and 007; references to  
  specs 002 to 007 resolve as those specs are written; alg none goes to spec    
  006 and balance overflow to spec 002; the freshness check of                  
  docs/traceability.md comes in the final phase, when the file is first         
  generated.                                                                    
      Run npm run check and npm run trace.

⏺ All 12 audit items are fixed. npm run check passes (32 unit tests), and npm 
  run trace exits 0 with 46 pending ACs across the two specs. Nothing is
  committed.

  Traceability checker, with a fixture case for each:
  1. Only tests Vitest runs count. The projects' file patterns now live in
     vitest.projects.ts, which both vitest.config.ts and the checker import. I
     didn't have the checker import vitest.config.ts directly, because that file
     also loads .env. Any *.test.ts that no project runs fails the check. That
     means the e2e tests for SYS-AC14 and SYS-AC17 will need an e2e project
     first.
  2. These no longer count as proof:
     - a test or suite with no function;
     - skip, todo or fails in an options object, which also covers the tests
       inside such a suite;
     - .fails;
     - an empty .each([]) table, which I confirmed runs zero tests without any
       warning.

     { skip: false }, a timeout argument and a function passed by name still
     count.
  3. Malformed specs are reported: a heading that looks like an AC but has the
     wrong format (### X-AC05:, ####, one digit, no space), with its line
     number, and an AC ID with another spec's prefix. A missing ID prefix line
     is reported too.
  4. Levels: only unit, integration, e2e and ci are accepted, and a missing
     Level line is reported. A Verified by line on a non-ci AC is reported.
  5. Code fences close only with the same character, at least as long. The
     fixture has a four-backtick fence around a three-backtick one, and a tilde
     fence holding backticks. I excluded that one fixture file from Prettier,
     which would otherwise rewrite the very fences under test.
  6. New fixture cases for the behaviours the audit listed as untested:
     - an undefined ID in a skipped test;
     - an unknown ID in a suite title;
     - .skipIf and .runIf still counting;
     - an empty table.
  7. Ticked tasks are enforced: an AC named by a - [x] task in any tasks.md must
     have a test, whatever the spec status. An undefined AC ID in a task fails
     the check. AGENTS.md now says to tick a task when it is done, and its
     description of npm run trace lists exactly what fails. specs/README.md
     documents all the rules.

  Specs
  8. SYS-AC16: the random source and the sleep are injected, so the test is
  deterministic. The backoff bounds are 10, 20, 40, 80, 160, 200 and 200 ms, and
  with the random source fixed at 0.5 the recorded delays are 5 ms, then 5 and
  10 ms. SYS-R18 now gives the exact formula.
  9. Timestamps and ids:
  - ACC-AC07 and ACC-AC11 now say updatedAt is "not earlier than" before the
    change.
  - Account and entry ids are UUIDv7, generated monotonically.
  - ACC-AC08, AC18 and AC19 assert the (createdAt, id) order instead of relying
    on distinct timestamps.
  10. Spec 000: operators deposit into "Any customer account, never a system
      account". SYS-R06 allows a minus sign only on amounts the API returns.
  11. Spec 001: the cursor now carries the user id. ACC-AC21 adds C2 using C1's
      cursor, which answers 400. New ACC-R27 and ACC-AC24: an operator listing
      accounts gets 403.
  12. /metrics: SYS-R01 drops /metrics from the endpoints that need no
      credentials. New SYS-R30 serves /metrics only on METRICS_PORT, which the
      load balancer never routes to. SYS-AC02 checks that it answers 200 there
      and 404 on the public port.

  The specs now stand at 30 requirements and 22 ACs for spec 000, and 27
  requirements and 24 ACs for spec 001.

  Left for later, as you triaged:
  - lock and statement timeouts, ordered timeouts, pool exhaustion and 429:
    specs 003 and 007;
  - references to specs 002–007, which resolve as those specs are written;
  - alg: none: spec 006;
  - balance overflow: spec 002;
  - the freshness check of docs/traceability.md: the final phase.

  METRICS_PORT and CURSOR_SECRET still need adding to .env.example when they're
  implemented.

✻ Brewed for 7m 21s · done 5:09 PM

❯ /audit                                                                        
  ⎿  Audit: phase/02-specs compared with main, plus uncommitted and untracked files

     This phase has no production code yet. It covers specs 000 and 001, the npm run trace gate (scripts/traceability.ts, vitest.projects.ts), its fixtures and tests, and the CI, AGENTS and dependency doc changes. I read everything in full. I checked by hand that every SYS and ACC requirement is covered by at least one AC, and it is. I did not run npm run trace or the tests, to keep the review read-only.

     Findings

     No Critical or High findings.

     Medium

     1. scripts/traceability.ts:465: the gate never checks an AC's Level, and a test only has to be included by some Vitest project
     - What is wrong: covered = tests.length > 0 for every AC that is not level ci. Any running test in any project counts as proof. The Level field is read but never used, and the gate cannot see whether a test actually ran or was skipped at runtime.
     - How to trigger it: SYS-AC10 is level integration (concurrent withdrawals against real Postgres). A unit test with a mocked repository named it('SYS-AC10 ...') makes it covered. Another way: add an e2e project to vitest.projects.ts without a CI step. SYS-AC14 and SYS-AC17 then show covered but never run in CI.
     - Impact: the concurrency and invariant ACs can be "proven" by tests that never touch the database. The gate exists to prevent exactly that.
     - Suggested fix: map each level to a project (unit to unit, integration to integration, e2e to e2e). Require at least one proving test in a file included by that project. Better still, read Vitest's JSON reporter output from the CI run, so only tests that actually ran and passed count.
     - AC: missing AC. Violates the rule in specs/README.md "Tests name the AC they prove" and AGENTS.md §10.

     2. scripts/traceability.ts:133-152: an unclosed code fence silently hides every AC after it
     - What is wrong: linesOutsideFences treats an unclosed fence as running to the end of the file, which is valid CommonMark. Nothing reports it.
     - How to trigger it: drop the closing backticks of the mermaid block at specs/001-accounts/spec.md:30-38. ACC-AC01 to ACC-AC24 disappear from the report. Once the spec is Implemented, an untested AC no longer fails the check.
     - Impact: the gate passes even though ACs are missing.
     - Suggested fix: if fence !== undefined at the end of the file, add a problem such as line N: code fence is never closed.
     - AC: missing AC (a gap in the gate's own rules).

     3. specs/001-accounts/spec.md:220-226 (ACC-AC15), specs/000-overview/spec.md:114-120 (SYS-AC01): the 403/404 order is unspecified, which leaves room for an existence oracle
     - What is wrong: AGENTS §3 says foreign resources answer 404. These ACs instead expect 403 for a customer acting on another customer's account (C1 freezing B1). That is safe only if the role check runs before the resource lookup. No requirement says so, and no AC uses an unknown id or a system account.
     - How to trigger it: an implementation that looks up the account first (404 if missing), then checks the role (403) passes ACC-AC15. A customer can then tell existing ids (403) from unknown ones (404) through the freeze, unfreeze, close, deposit and reverse endpoints.
     - Impact: account and transaction ids can be enumerated, against a non-negotiable. UUIDv7 randomness limits the practical risk, but the ids also leak timestamps.
     - Suggested fix: add a requirement to 000: "a role check precedes any resource lookup, so a forbidden role gets 403 whatever the id". Extend ACC-AC15 and SYS-AC01 with an unknown id U, the system account S and an unknown transaction id, all expecting identical 403 bodies.
     - AC: ACC-AC15, SYS-AC01; SYS-R04/SYS-R05 and AGENTS §3 Security.

     4. specs/000-overview/spec.md:128 (SYS-AC02) contradicts SYS-R01/SYS-R02 (:79-80)
     - What is wrong: R01 requires authentication on every public-port request except health and docs. R02 then requires 401 for a request without credentials. SYS-AC02 lists "/metrics on the public port answers 404" among the requests sent without credentials.
     - How to trigger it: with the usual global Fastify onRequest auth hook (it also runs for the not-found handler), an unauthenticated GET /metrics on the public port answers 401. That satisfies R02 and fails SYS-AC02.
     - Impact: the implementer has to settle the conflict silently, which AGENTS §2 forbids.
     - Suggested fix: pick one. Either SYS-AC02 sends the public /metrics request with valid credentials and expects 404, or R01 is scoped to defined routes so unknown paths answer 404 before auth. This changes a requirement, so the owner must approve.
     - AC: SYS-AC02.

     5. specs/000-overview/spec.md:100 (SYS-R22): an idempotent replay contradicts the "requestId in every problem body" rule
     - What is wrong: a replay must return the stored status and body, and stored business rejections (422) are problem details. Their requestId belongs to the original request. SYS-R22 demands the correlation id of the request being handled, which is the one in the current X-Request-Id header.
     - How to trigger it: C1 withdraws too much with key k1 and X-Request-Id: a (422 stored). C1 retries with k1 and X-Request-Id: b. Either the body says requestId: "a", which breaks SYS-R22, or it is rewritten to "b", which breaks byte-identical replay. SYS-AC14's "both k1 requests return the same response" doesn't settle it either, because the X-Request-Id headers differ.
     - Impact: specs 000 and 005 will conflict. Tests can pass or fail depending on which reading the implementer picks.
     - Suggested fix: state in 000 that a replay returns the stored body unchanged, requestId included. The response header carries the current X-Request-Id, and SYS-R22 makes an exception for replays. Optionally add an Idempotent-Replayed: true header. Make SYS-AC14 compare status and body only.
     - AC: SYS-R22, SYS-AC14; spec 005 to follow.

     6. specs/000-overview/spec.md:303 and :103 (SYS-R25): lock and statement timeouts fall through to 500
     - What is wrong: the shared error catalogue gives 503 only for 40P01/40001 after three attempts. Under SYS-R25, a lock_timeout (55P03), a statement_timeout (57014) or pool exhaustion must answer 500 /problems/internal-error. Nothing defines the separate account-lock-timeout setting (503) and idempotency-wait-timeout setting (409). Nothing orders the timeouts as database < service < load balancer, and graceful shutdown isn't mentioned.
     - How to trigger it: hold A1's row lock in another session longer than lock_timeout, then withdraw from A1. The only applicable rule today is 500.
     - Impact: a transient, retryable condition is reported as an internal error without Retry-After. Clients and the load balancer can't tell it apart from a bug.
     - Suggested fix: in 000 (or a forward reference to 003, 005 and 007), add a requirement and AC: account lock timeout → 503 /problems/service-unavailable with Retry-After. Add separate configurable timeouts with the ordering above, plus the 409 idempotency-wait answer, as AGENTS and spec 005 intend.
     - AC: missing AC.

     7. specs/000-overview/spec.md:79-80, 122-128: token validation is underspecified
     - What is wrong: SYS-AC02 covers only "no token", "expired" and "bad signature". Nothing defines the answer for an alg: none or non-HS256 token, a token with no exp, a missing sub, or a role claim that is missing or outside customer/operator.
     - How to trigger it: a correctly signed token with role: "admin" or no role claim. The specs don't say whether it gets 401 or 403, or how it is treated.
     - Impact: algorithm confusion and role-claim mistakes are the classic JWT authorization bypasses. As written, nothing would catch them.
     - Suggested fix: add a requirement that pins the algorithm and requires exp, sub and a role claim from the closed set, with anything else answering 401 /problems/unauthenticated. Extend SYS-AC02 with those tokens.
     - AC: SYS-AC02 / missing AC.

     8. specs/001-accounts/spec.md:337, 346, 97 (Q4, Q13, ACC-R22): the timestamp precision of the cursor is unspecified
     - What is wrong: the cursor carries createdAt, and the API writes timestamps with millisecond precision (Q13). PostgreSQL timestamptz stores microseconds. If the cursor holds the millisecond value, (created_at, id) < (cursor_ts, cursor_id) compares truncated values against full-precision rows.
     - How to trigger it: two entries in the same millisecond with different microseconds, on a page boundary. One is repeated or skipped. ACC-AC20 uses identical timestamps, so it can't detect this.
     - Impact: history pages skip or repeat ledger entries, which ACC-R22 forbids.
     - Suggested fix: state that the cursor carries the stored precision (microseconds), or that created_at is stored truncated to milliseconds. Add a case to ACC-AC20 with entries 0.4 ms apart in the same millisecond.
     - AC: ACC-AC20 / ACC-R22.

     Low

     9. scripts/traceability.ts:239: a spec folder without spec.md is dropped silently, including its tasks.md
     - How to trigger it: commit specs/003-movements/Spec.md. On macOS (case-insensitive) existsSync finds it; on Linux CI the whole spec, its ACs and its ticked tasks vanish and the check passes.
     - Suggested fix: report any NNN-* folder without spec.md as a problem.
     - AC: missing AC.

     10. scripts/traceability.ts:576-579: the entry-point guard fails open
     - What is wrong: if resolve(argv[1]) differs from the realpath in import.meta.url (for example a symlinked path), the script exits 0 with no output, and the CI step passes.
     - Suggested fix: use import.meta.main (available on Node 24) or a separate CLI file. Add a test that spawns the script on the failing fixture and expects exit 1.
     - AC: missing AC.

     11. scripts/traceability.ts:304, 322-326: some forms count as proof when they shouldn't
     - Template placeholders are joined with '', so `SYS-AC0${x}1` reads as SYS-AC01.
     - it('SYS-AC01', opts) with opts as a variable counts as having a handler, but Vitest treats it as todo.
     - it('…', opts, fn) with opts = { skip: true } held in a variable counts as proof.
     - it.skipIf(true) written as a literal counts as proof.
     - A test inside a function that is never called, or inside if (false), counts as proof.
     - Suggested fix: join template parts with a separator character, and treat non-literal options as not proof. Item 1's JSON-results fix removes most of these.
     - AC: missing AC.

     12. scripts/traceability.ts:210-218, 427: two task and prefix rules aren't checked
     - A ticked task whose AC IDs sit on a wrapped continuation line isn't enforced.
     - The rule "ID prefix unique across specs" (specs/README.md:32) isn't checked: two specs with prefix MOV and different AC numbers pass.
     - AC: missing AC.

     13. specs/000-overview/spec.md:151, 167, 271 (SYS-AC05, SYS-AC07, SYS-AC20): the order between 400 and 422 is undefined
     - What is wrong: these deposits expect 422 but don't mention an Idempotency-Key. SYS-R26 says a missing key is 400.
     - How to trigger it: an implementation that validates headers first answers 400 and fails these ACs.
     - Suggested fix: add "with a fresh Idempotency-Key" to each, and state the validation order.
     - AC: SYS-AC05, SYS-AC07, SYS-AC20.

     14. specs/000-overview/spec.md:183, 270 (SYS-AC09, SYS-AC20): test seams have no production guard
     - What is wrong: these ACs need a fault-injection hook and a route that throws. Nothing requires those to be absent from production builds.
     - Suggested fix: state that they are registered only in the test app instance, and add an assertion that the production app has no such route.
     - AC: SYS-AC09, SYS-AC20.

     15. specs/000-overview/spec.md:33: no answer is defined for an operator depositing into a system account
     - What is wrong: the role table says "never a system account", but no requirement or AC says what that request gets. ACC-AC22 (specs/001-accounts/spec.md:282) asserts operator 404 on reading S, but no requirement covers it. It only covers ACC-R25, which is about customer endpoints.
     - Suggested fix: add requirements and ACs for operator 404 on system accounts: read, deposit and status changes.
     - AC: missing AC; ACC-AC22.

     16. specs/000-overview/spec.md:51, 90: cached balance overflow is undefined
     - What is wrong: the per-movement maximum doesn't bound the cached balance. When it passes 2^63−1, PostgreSQL raises 22003, which falls to a 500 under SYS-R25.
     - Suggested fix: define a 422 problem type for it (in the ledger spec).
     - AC: missing AC.

     17. specs/000-overview/spec.md:30-38 and :61: the role table and glossary are incomplete
     - Table 1.1 has no rows for "List own accounts" or "List history". By SYS-R03, no role is permitted them, yet ACC-R08 and ACC-R27 define them.
     - The glossary defines an audit record as covering money movements only, while ACC-R26 also audits status changes.
     - SYS-AC01 sends an Idempotency-Key on freeze, unfreeze and close, but ACC Q5 says these take none. Nothing says whether a key sent anyway is ignored or rejected.
     - AC: SYS-AC01, ACC-R26.

     Categories without findings

     - Money: no number or float on amounts anywhere. SYS-R06, SYS-R07 and SYS-AC06 specify digit strings, bigint and the currency table with JPY exponent 0. Only Low item 16 applies.
     - Transactions and concurrency: no findings. SYS-R11, R17, R18 and R19 and ACC-R17 match AGENTS §3 (one transaction, ordered FOR UPDATE, system accounts never locked, checks after locks, bounded retry). The backoff values in SYS-AC16 are arithmetically correct.
     - Tests: no sleeps or timing assumptions. Fixtures use temporary directories and clean them up. The gaps are in what the gate accepts as proof (items 1, 2, 9-12), not in the tests themselves.
     - Docs drift: AGENTS.md, specs/README.md, docs/dependencies.md and the CI step match the new script's behaviour. Only the spec-internal items 15 and 17 apply.

     Verdict: fix before shipping

❯ Triage of the second audit. Fix now.                                          
  Traceability gate, a change of approach: stop treating test source text as    
  proof. Coverage comes from what Vitest actually ran.                          
  1. Each test script writes Vitest's JSON report for its project:              
  reports/vitest-unit.json, reports/vitest-integration.json and, later,         
  reports/vitest-e2e.json. reports/ is git-ignored.                             
  2. npm run trace reads the specs, the tasks.md files and those reports. An AC 
  is covered only by a test that passed, whose full name contains the AC ID,    
  in the report of the project that matches its Level (unit, integration or     
  e2e). Level ci keeps the Verified by rule. A test that names an undefined AC  
  fails the gate whatever its status.                                           
  3. A missing report means that project was not run: its ACs show as not       
  verified, and fail only when that project is required with --require. CI runs 
  trace after the unit and integration tests with --require unit,integration.   
  4. Remove the static parsing of test files and the tests that only covered    
  it. Keep the spec parsing, and also fail on: an unclosed code fence, a        
  specs/NNN-* folder without spec.md, a ticked task whose AC IDs are not on its 
  checkbox line, and an ID prefix used by two specs.                            
  5. Move the command line into its own file, so a failing check can never exit 
  0 by skipping main, and add a test that runs it on the failing fixture and    
  expects exit 1.                                                               
     Specs:                                                                     
  6. Spec 000 states the order of checks in one requirement: authentication     
  (401), role (403), malformed request (400), validation (422), resource lookup 
  and ownership (404), then business rules (409 or 422). A forbidden role gets  
  403 whatever the id, so a customer cannot learn which ids exist. Extend       
  ACC-AC15 and SYS-AC01 with an unknown id, a system account and an unknown     
  transaction id, all with identical 403 bodies. ACs that expect 422 on a       
  movement send a fresh Idempotency-Key.                                        
  7. Authentication applies to defined routes only; an unknown path answers 404 
  with or without credentials. Adjust SYS-R01 and SYS-AC02.                     
  8. An idempotent replay returns the stored status and body unchanged,         
  including the original requestId; the response carries the current            
  X-Request-Id header plus Idempotent-Replayed: true. SYS-R22 names this        
  exception, and SYS-AC14 compares status and body.                             
  9. The shared error catalogue lists the conditions that later specs answer,   
  so none falls to 500: account lock timeout 503 with Retry-After (spec 003),   
  idempotency wait timeout 409 (spec 005), pool exhausted 503 and rate limit    
  429 (spec 007), with timeouts ordered database < service < load balancer.     
  10. Token rules in spec 000, detailed later in spec 006: the algorithm is     
  pinned to HS256, and exp, sub and a role from the closed set are required;    
  anything else answers 401 /problems/unauthenticated. Extend SYS-AC02 with alg 
  none, a missing role and an unknown role.                                     
  11. The cursor carries created_at at the stored precision, microseconds. Add  
  a case to ACC-AC20 with two entries 0.4 ms apart in the same millisecond.     
  12. The fault-injection hook and the throwing route exist only in the test    
  app, and an AC checks that the production app has neither.                    
  13. An operator gets 404 for a system account on read, deposit and status     
  changes.                                                                      
  14. Table 1.1 gets rows for listing own accounts and listing history; the     
  glossary's audit record also covers status changes; an Idempotency-Key sent   
  to an endpoint that takes none is ignored.                                    
      Leave for later: balance overflow goes to spec 002.                       
      Run npm run check, npm run test:integration and npm run trace.

⏺ Vitest's JSON report can't distinguish .fails tests from passed ones, so to
  enforce that .fails doesn't count as proof, I'll add an ESLint rule banning
  .fails and fails: in test files. Now I'll write the new library.

      470 +/** Parses the command line; returns an error message for bad 
          +usage. */                                                     
      471 +export function parseArgs(args: readonly string[]): Options | 
          +string {                                                      
      472 +  const options: Options = { write: false, require: [] };     
      473 +  for (let index = 0; index < args.length; index += 1) {      
      474 +    const arg = args[index] ?? '';                            
      475 +    if (arg === '--write') {                                  
      476 +      options.write = true;                                   
      477 +      continue;                                               
      478 +    }                                                         
      479 +    const inline = /^--require=(.*)$/.exec(arg)?.[1];         
      480 +    const value = inline ?? (arg === '--require' ? args[(index
          + += 1)] : undefined);                                         
      481 +    if (value === undefined) return `Unknown argument: ${arg}`
          +;                                                             
      482 +    for (const name of value.split(',')) {                    
      483 +      if (!isProject(name)) return `Unknown project for --requ
          +ire: "${name}"`;                                              
      484 +      options.require.push(name);                             
      485 +    }                                                         
      486 +  }                                                           
      487 +  return options;                                             
      488 +}

  ⎿  Updated specs/README.md (+23 -14)
       66  it('MOV-AC03 rejects a withdrawal larger than the balance', as
           ync () => { ... });
       67  ```
       68  
       69 -`npm run trace` parses every `*.test.ts` file that a Vitest pr
          -oject runs (the include globs in `vitest.projects.ts`) and rea
          -ds the titles of `describe`, `suite`, `it` and `test` calls, i
          -ncluding `.each` and `.for` (table-driven tests, with any of `
          -.skip`, `.only`, `.todo` and `.concurrent`), `.skipIf` and `.r
          -unIf`.                                                        
       69 +Proof comes from what Vitest actually ran, never from the test
          + source. `npm test` and `npm run test:integration` each write 
          +a JSON report (`reports/vitest-unit.json`, `reports/vitest-int
          +egration.json`, and later `reports/vitest-e2e.json`), and `npm
          + run trace` reads them.                                       
       70  
       71 -- A test's name is its `describe` titles plus its own title, s
          -o an ID in a `describe` title covers every running test inside
          - it. A table-driven test counts when the ID is in its title te
          -mplate, for example `it.each(cases)('MOV-AC07 rejects %s', ...
          -)`.                                                           
       72 -- These never count as proof: a test marked `.skip`, `.todo` o
          -r `.fails`, or with `skip`, `todo` or `fails` in its options o
          -bject; a test with no function, which Vitest treats as todo; a
          - test generated from an empty table such as `it.each([])`; any
          - test inside a suite that is skipped, todo or fails in the sam
          -e ways; and a `describe` with no running test inside. `.skipIf
          -` and `.runIf` still count.                                   
       73 -- IDs in comments, assertions, `.each` tables or other strings
          - do not count.                                                
       74 -- An ID that no spec defines fails the check wherever it appea
          -rs in a name, even in a skipped test.                         
       75 -- A `*.test.ts` file that no Vitest project runs fails the che
          -ck, because its tests would never run. A new kind of test, suc
          -h as e2e, needs its project first.                            
       76 -- `test/fixtures/` is ignored: it holds sample specs and tests
          - for the checker itself.                                      
       71 +- An AC is covered only by a test that **passed**, whose full 
          +name (its `describe` titles plus its own title) contains the A
          +C ID, in the report of the project that matches the AC's `Leve
          +l`. A unit test never proves an integration AC.               
       72 +- Skipped, todo and failed tests prove nothing, and neither do
          +es a test that never ran. A table-driven test counts through e
          +ach generated case, for example `it.each(cases)('MOV-AC07 reje
          +cts %s', ...)`.

⏺ Every item of the second triage is done. npm run check passes (28 unit tests),
  npm run test:integration passes (3 tests), and npm run trace -- --require 
  unit,integration exits 0. It reports 50 ACs: 48 pending, plus 2 unverified
  (SYS-AC14 and SYS-AC17, whose e2e project doesn't exist yet). Nothing is
  committed.

  Traceability gate: coverage now comes from what Vitest ran
  1. Reports: npm test and npm run test:integration write
     reports/vitest-unit.json and reports/vitest-integration.json. The
     .gitignore entry is /reports/, root only, so the fixtures keep their sample
     reports.
  2. What counts: an AC is covered only by a passing test whose full name
     contains its ID, in the report matching its Level. A unit test never proves
     an integration AC. An undefined ID fails the gate whatever the test's
     status.
  3. Missing reports: an AC whose project has no report shows as unverified, and
     fails only when --require names that project. CI now runs npm run trace --
     --require unit,integration after the integration tests instead of after
     npm run check.
  4. Static parsing removed: the TypeScript test-file parser,
     vitest.projects.ts, the test-source fixtures and their tests are gone.
     vitest.config.ts and docs/dependencies.md are back to their state on main.
     The gate now also fails on:
     - an unclosed code fence;
     - a specs/NNN-* folder without an exactly named spec.md (the fixture uses
       Spec.md, which macOS would otherwise accept);
     - AC IDs on a ticked task's continuation line;
     - an ID prefix used by two specs;
     - a malformed report.
  5. CLI: it lives in scripts/trace.ts with no entry-point guard. A test runs it
     as a separate process and expects exit 1 on the failing fixture and 0 on
     the passing one.

  One addition you didn't ask for. Vitest reports a .fails test as passed even
  though its body failed, so the report can't tell it apart from real proof. To
  keep your earlier rule that .fails is not proof, ESLint now bans .fails and
  fails: options in *.test.ts files. I checked the rule against a probe file.
  Say if you'd rather drop it.

  Specs
  - Spec 000 now has 39 requirements and 26 ACs:
    - SYS-R31: checks run in a fixed order, unknown route first, and a forbidden
      role gets 403 whatever the id. SYS-AC23 proves each step in order.
      SYS-AC01 adds an unknown id, the settlement account and an unknown
      transaction, all with identical 403 bodies.
    - Unknown paths: SYS-R01 now authenticates defined routes only, and SYS-R32
      answers 404 for an unknown path with or without credentials. SYS-AC02
      checks both.
    - Replays: SYS-R33 returns the stored status and body unchanged, with the
      current X-Request-Id and Idempotent-Replayed: true. SYS-R22 names the
      exception, and SYS-AC14 compares status and body.
    - Transient conditions: SYS-R34 plus four new catalogue rows: lock timeout
      503 (spec 003), idempotency wait timeout 409 (spec 005), exhausted pool
      503 and rate limit 429 (spec 007). SYS-R35 orders the timeouts. Their ACs
      come in those specs.
    - Tokens: SYS-R36 pins HS256 and requires exp, sub and a valid role.
      SYS-AC02 adds alg: none, a missing role and an unknown role.
    - Test seams: SYS-R37 and SYS-AC24 keep the fault-injection hook and the
      throwing route out of the production app.
    - Operators and system accounts: SYS-R38 and SYS-AC25 answer 404 on read,
      deposit and status changes.
    - Ignored key: SYS-R39 and SYS-AC26 ignore an Idempotency-Key sent to an
      endpoint that takes none.
    - Smaller edits: SYS-AC05, AC07, AC19 and AC20 send a fresh key. The role
      table gains rows for listing accounts and listing history, and the
      glossary's audit record covers status changes.
  - Spec 001 still has 27 requirements and 24 ACs:
    - The cursor carries createdAt in microseconds (ACC-R22, Q4, Q13). ACC-AC20
      adds two entries 0.4 ms apart within the same millisecond.
    - ACC-AC15 adds the settlement account and an unknown id: twelve identical
      403s. ACC-AC14's movements carry fresh keys.
    - Q11 is marked decided, pointing to SYS-R38.

    I didn't add the unknown transaction id to ACC-AC15, since status changes
    take no transaction. SYS-AC01 covers it through a reversal.

  Docs: specs/README.md and AGENTS.md describe the report-based gate exactly:
  the order to run things in, the four coverage states, --require, and every
  failure condition.

  Left for later:
  - Balance overflow goes to spec 002, as you triaged.
  - /ship still runs npm run trace without --require. CI enforces it, so I left
    the skill alone, but say if /ship should pass --require unit,integration
    too.

  ⎿  Audit: phase/02-specs vs main (including uncommitted and untracked files)

     This phase adds no service code.
