     1. A required AC whose test report is missing never fails the gate in CI
     - Where: scripts/traceability.ts:384-385 and listFailures at 408-409; .github/workflows/ci.yml:68; .claude/skills/ship/SKILL.md:31
     - What is wrong: If the report for an AC's level is missing, the AC is marked unverified, even when its spec is Implemented or a ticked task names it. unverified fails only when --require names that project. CI passes --require unit,integration, so e2e reports are never required. /ship runs npm run trace with no --require at all.
     - How to trigger: Set 000-overview to Implemented and run CI. SYS-AC14 (any replica gives the same result) and SYS-AC17 (load test) have no e2e report. Both show unverified and CI stays green.
     - Impact: The e2e proof of replica-safe idempotency is never enforced. It also contradicts AGENTS.md:80, which says the gate "Fails when an AC of an Implemented spec … has no passing test in the report of its level".
     - Suggested fix: Treat "required and unverified" as missing, with the reason "report absent". Alternatively, derive the required projects from the levels of required ACs. Either way, make /ship pass the same --require as CI.
     - AC: missing AC for the gate itself (AGENTS.md §5).

     2. One passing test covers an AC even when its other tests are skipped or failed
     - Where: scripts/traceability.ts:365-372
     - What is wrong: An AC is covered as soon as any test naming it passes. Skipped, todo or failed tests naming the same AC are ignored.
     - How to trigger: Write it('SYS-AC10 exactly 3 of 10 withdrawals succeed') and next to it it.skip('SYS-AC10 cached balance cannot be set to -1'). Vitest exits 0 with skipped tests, and the gate reports SYS-AC10 as covered.
     - Impact: The invariant half of an AC, here the database check that balances never go negative, can be switched off silently while CI stays green.
     - Suggested fix: For a required AC, fail when any test naming it has a status other than passed.
     - AC: missing AC for the gate (specs/README.md "Tests name the AC they prove").

     3. The token rules of SYS-R36 are only half covered by an AC
     - Where: specs/000-overview/spec.md:116, with SYS-AC02 at 138-139
     - What is wrong: SYS-R36 requires a 401 for tokens that lack exp or sub, and for any algorithm other than HS256. SYS-AC02 covers only expired tokens, bad signatures, alg: none, a missing role and role: admin.
     - How to trigger: An implementation that accepts a token with no exp, or an HS512/RS256 token, still passes every AC.
     - Impact: Tokens that never expire, or algorithm-confusion attacks, would go unproven. Spec 006, which should detail this, does not exist yet.
     - Suggested fix: Add the no-exp, no-sub, HS512 and RS256 cases to SYS-AC02, or add a placeholder in spec 006 with these cases.
     - AC: SYS-AC02 / SYS-R36.

     4. SYS-R11 and SYS-R12 contradict storing business rejections for idempotent replay
     - Where: specs/000-overview/spec.md:91-92
     - What is wrong: SYS-R11 says a movement's ledger entries, balance changes, idempotency record and audit record are "either all committed or all absent". SYS-R12 says a rejected movement applies "none of its effects". But a stored business rejection (for example a 422 for insufficient funds, the behaviour expected of spec 005 and recommended by Q7) is an idempotency record committed with no entries.
     - How to trigger: Write spec 005 as intended. It then contradicts R11 as written, and because "the spec wins", tests for SYS-AC10 ("7 answer 422 without effects") can disagree on whether the stored key row counts as an effect.
     - Impact: Idempotency semantics stay ambiguous on the money path.
     - Suggested fix: Limit R11's "all or nothing" to applied movements. In R12, change "effects" to "ledger and balance effects", and leave the idempotency record to spec 005.
     - AC: SYS-AC09, SYS-AC10 / SYS-R11, SYS-R12.

     Low

     5. The ESLint ban on .fails is easy to bypass and has no test
     - Where: eslint.config.js:73-79
     - What is wrong: The rule only catches it/test/describe/suite by name and options passed as an object literal. Vitest's typings confirm that skipIf()/runIf() and test.extend() return an API that still has .fails.
     - How to trigger: const dbTest = test.extend({...}); dbTest.fails('MOV-AC03 …'), or it.skipIf(false).fails(...), import { it as t }, const o = { fails: true }; it('x', o, fn), or { 'fails': true }. The rule also gives false positives such as toEqual({ fails: 1 }).
     - Impact: A test that passes only when its body fails can prove an AC. test.extend is the likely way database fixtures will be written from phase 05 on.
     - Suggested fix: Add a Vitest setup file with beforeEach(({ task }) => { if (task.fails) throw … }), so the check runs at runtime whatever the syntax, and test it.
     - AC: missing AC.

     6. Some valid Markdown task forms are silently not enforced
     - Where: scripts/traceability.ts:28-29 and 233-247
     - What is wrong: Tasks in ordered lists (1. [x] T1 SYS-AC01) are not recognised. AC IDs on a continuation line after a blank line, or on an unindented (lazy) continuation line, are dropped without a problem. specs/README.md:99 says they fail the check.
     - How to trigger: Tick 1. [x] T1 build SYS-AC04 and the AC is not enforced.
     - Impact: A ticked task's ACs can escape the gate.
     - Suggested fix: Accept \d+[.)] list markers, and report any AC ID in tasks.md that is not on a checkbox line.
     - AC: missing AC.

     7. Old test reports are read as if they were fresh
     - Where: scripts/traceability.ts:312-328
     - What is wrong: reports/*.json stay on disk between runs and nothing checks how old they are.
     - How to trigger: Run npm test after breaking an integration test, then npm run trace. The integration report from an earlier run still proves the ticked AC.
     - Impact: Only local runs and /ship are affected; CI starts from a fresh checkout.
     - Suggested fix: Compare each report's startTime with the newest test file's mtime and fail or warn, or add a script that runs both test projects and then the trace.
     - AC: missing AC.

     8. A Verified by: line counts as proof whatever it names
     - Where: scripts/traceability.ts:383
     - What is wrong: For level ci, any non-empty Verified by: line makes the AC covered.
     - How to trigger: - **Verified by:** CI job \terraform`with no such job inci.yml` is reported as covered.
     - Impact: CI-level ACs can be marked proven without any check behind them.
     - Suggested fix: Require the value to name a job or command present in .github/workflows/ci.yml.
     - AC: missing AC.

     9. Operator account opening is still "Pending" in table 1.1
     - Where: specs/000-overview/spec.md:40
     - What is wrong: Table 1.1 lists operator account opening as "Pending (spec 001 Q1)". But ACC-R06 and ACC-AC06 already require a 403, and SYS-R03 defines permissions from that table.
     - How to trigger: Read the two specs side by side; they answer differently.
     - Impact: The two specs disagree about who may open an account.
     - Suggested fix: Change the cell to "No (spec 001 Q1)".
     - AC: SYS-AC01 / ACC-AC06.

     10. No requirement covers a balance that would overflow bigint
     - Where: specs/000-overview/spec.md:53, with SYS-R07 at 87
     - What is wrong: The limits apply per movement only. A credit that would push a cached balance past 9223372036854775807 is not handled by any requirement.
     - How to trigger: Deposit into an account near the maximum. PostgreSQL raises 22003 and SYS-R25 turns it into a 500.
     - Impact: A movement could answer 500 instead of a defined rejection.
     - Suggested fix: Add a requirement and an AC answering 422 with a defined problem type, in the ledger or movements spec.
     - AC: missing AC.

     11. ACC-R26 does not say whether a no-op status change is audited
     - Where: specs/001-accounts/spec.md:101
     - What is wrong: It is unclear whether a status change that changes nothing (ACC-R15) writes an audit record, and ACC-AC23 does not test that case.
     - How to trigger: Freeze an account that is already frozen. Either behaviour satisfies the spec.
     - Impact: Two implementations can differ and both pass.
     - Suggested fix: State the rule and add the case to ACC-AC23.
     - AC: ACC-AC23.

     12. The CLI test may time out on a slow CI runner
     - Where: test/unit/traceability.test.ts:272-278
     - What is wrong: The test starts two cold tsx processes inside Vitest's default 5 s timeout (the unit project sets no testTimeout).
     - How to trigger: Run it on a slow or busy CI runner.
     - Impact: Occasional false failures.
     - Suggested fix: Give this test an explicit timeout, for example 20 s.
     - AC: n/a.

     13. docs/traceability.md can go stale without anyone noticing
     - Where: scripts/traceability.ts:13 and .prettierignore:9
     - What is wrong: --write generates the file from local, possibly stale, reports. It is not gitignored and CI does not check it.
     - How to trigger: Commit it once, then change the tests.
     - Impact: A committed copy no longer matches the code.
     - Suggested fix: Either gitignore it, or regenerate it in CI and fail if it differs.
     - AC: n/a.

     Critical files

     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/scripts/traceability.ts
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/eslint.config.js
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/.github/workflows/ci.yml

     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/.claude/skills/ship/SKILL.md

     Categories without findings

     - Spec conformance: none beyond the above. Every SYS and ACC requirement is covered by at least one AC.
     - Money: no findings. There is no amount code yet, and the specs use bigint, digit strings and the currency table consistently.
     - Transactions and concurrency: no findings. There is no code yet, and the specs match AGENTS.md (ordered locks, system accounts never locked, bounded retries in SYS-R18 and SYS-AC16).
     - Failure handling: no findings. Timeout ordering and the 409/503 split are stated in SYS-R34, SYS-R35 and the error catalogue.

     Verdict: fix before shipping

⏺ Typecheck fails because Vitest types task.fails as read-only. Clearing it is
  the whole trick, so I'll cast away the readonly with a comment saying why:

⏺ Update(test/setup/forbid-fails.ts)
  ⎿  Added 3 lines, removed 1 line
       6  // extended test. The flag is cleared first: with it set, Vites
          t would turn this error into a pass.
       7  beforeEach(({ task }) => {
       8    if (task.fails === true) {
       9 -    task.fails = false;                                        
       9 +    // Vitest types the flag as read-only, but the runner reads
         + it only after the test ends, so                               
      10 +    // clearing it here is what makes the error below count as 
         +a failure.

      13        'Tests marked fails are forbidden: they pass when their b
          ody fails, so they cannot prove an AC.',

  ⎿  (timeout 5m)

⏺ All 11 items of the third triage are fixed, and the /audit verdict rule is
  updated. npm run check passes (29 unit tests), npm run test:integration passes
  (3 tests), and npm run trace -- --require unit,integration exits 0. It
  reports 50 ACs: 48 pending and 2 e2e ACs unverified. Nothing is committed.

  Gate and tooling
  1. Same gate everywhere: /ship now runs npm run trace -- --require 
     unit,integration, exactly like CI. AGENTS.md and specs/README.md say that
     e2e ACs show as unverified, and aren't enforced, until phase 12, when CI
     runs the e2e suite and requires its report.
  2. Tests that didn't pass: for a required AC, any test naming it that is
     skipped, todo or failed fails the gate, even if another test naming it
     passed. The failing fixture adds an AC with one passing and one skipped
     test. The passing fixture keeps a skipped test on a Draft AC, which doesn't
     fail.
  3. fails tests: the ESLint rule is gone (eslint.config.js is back to its state
     on main). The new test/setup/forbid-fails.ts runs in both test projects
     and fails any test marked fails at runtime. A unit test runs Vitest on a
     sample file and checks five forms: the modifier, an option, a suite's
     options, test.extend, and .skipIf(false).fails. A normal test still passes.

     One subtlety: the hook has to clear the flag before throwing, or Vitest
     would turn the error into a pass. Vitest types that flag as read-only, so
     the code casts it, with a comment explaining why.
  4. Task lines: only - [ ] and - [x] are tasks. * [x], 1. [x] and - [X] are
     reported, and so is any AC ID in tasks.md that isn't on a task's checkbox
     line. That replaces the old continuation-line rule.
  5. Small fixes: the CLI test has a 20 s timeout. docs/traceability.md is
     git-ignored, and its now-redundant .prettierignore entry is removed.

  Specs
  - SYS-AC02 adds tokens without exp, without sub, and signed with HS512 and
    with RS256. All answer 401.
  - SYS-R11 now applies its all-or-nothing rule to movements that are applied.
    SYS-R12 says a rejected movement has no ledger or balance effects. What a
    rejection stores is left to spec 005. SYS-AC10's wording matches.
  - Table 1.1: an operator opening an account is now "No (spec 001 Q1)".
  - New SYS-R40 plus a catalogue row: a credit that would overflow a cached
    balance is rejected as spec 002 defines, never with 500. Spec 000 now has 40
    requirements.
  - ACC-R26: a status change that changes nothing writes no audit record.
    ACC-AC23 adds the second freeze.

  Audit skill:
  - The verdict is "fix before shipping" only for a Critical or High finding, or
    a Medium one about money, concurrency, idempotency or security. Otherwise
    it's "ready to ship", with a Follow-ups: line.
  - Anything a spec, plan or task already assigns to later work is listed once
    as Deferred, already planned: ... rather than reported as a finding.

  Not done, as you said: a check for stale local reports, and a check that a
  Verified by line names a real CI job. The latter comes with the first ci-level
  ACs in phase 12.

✻ Sautéed for 4m 8s · done 5:40 PM

❯ /audit                                                                        
  ⎿  No Critical, High or Medium findings. The traceability gate is strict, and specs 000 and 001 agree with each other and with AGENTS.md. Everything below is Low.

     1. Low: a test report can be out of date or labelled with the wrong level, and it still counts as proof
     - scripts/traceability.ts:313-327
     - What is wrong: loadReports only checks that reports/vitest-<project>.json exists. It labels every result in the file with the project in the file name. It never checks when the report was written or which project actually ran each test file.
     - How to trigger it:
       - Out of date: run npm run test:integration, then break an integration test, run only npm test, then npm run trace. The old integration report still marks the AC covered.
       - Wrong level: npm run test:integration -- --project unit writes unit test results into vitest-integration.json. A unit test named SYS-AC09 ... then proves an integration AC.
     - Impact: only the local workflow in AGENTS.md:23 (run the tests, then the gate) is affected. An agent can tick a task on false proof. CI and /ship rerun both suites first, so the real gate is not affected. Still, it breaks the README's promise (specs/README.md:69) that proof comes only from what Vitest actually ran.
     - Suggested fix: reject a report that contains a file outside its project's folders (test/unit/, src/ for unit; test/integration/ for integration). Print each report's startTime in the summary, or fail when the report is older than the newest spec or test file.
     - AC: missing AC (tooling)

     2. Low: an AC ID mentioned anywhere in a test name counts as proof of that AC
     - scripts/traceability.ts:369-376
     - What is wrong: every AC ID in a passing test's full name counts as proven, not only the AC the test is about.
     - How to trigger it: SYS-AC15's Then clause says "SYS-AC11 and SYS-AC12 hold". A natural test name is it('SYS-AC15 crossed transfers end balanced, SYS-AC11 and SYS-AC12 hold'). That test alone marks SYS-AC11 and SYS-AC12 covered, because they are also integration level.
     - Impact: an invariant AC can look proven by a test that never checks it.
     - Suggested fix: count only the first AC ID in the test's own title, or in its nearest describe, as proof. Treat any other ID as a mention, and still check that it exists.
     - AC: missing AC (tooling)

     3. Low: the specs don't say which answer a transfer to an account the customer cannot credit gets
     - specs/000-overview/spec.md:111 (SYS-R31), with specs/000-overview/spec.md:117 (SYS-R05) and specs/001-accounts/spec.md:94,234,341 (ACC-R19, ACC-AC16, Q8)
     - What is wrong:
       - SYS-R31 puts the account lookup (404) before business rules (422).
       - SYS-R05 lists 404 only for the source side.
       - ACC-AC16 fixes 422 for a transfer into another customer's frozen account.
       - Q8 says that answer must be the same as for a destination that does not exist.
       - Nothing says the destination check is a business rule rather than a lookup.
     - How to trigger it: spec 003 follows SYS-R31 literally and answers an unknown destination with 404. ACC-AC16 then contradicts Q8, or a customer can tell a frozen foreign account (422) from a missing one (404).
     - Impact: it can reveal another customer's account status once spec 003 is written. Today it is only an ambiguity.
     - Suggested fix: state in SYS-R31 or SYS-R05 that the lookup step covers only the account the caller acts on. A destination that is unknown, foreign and frozen or closed, or a system account is refused with 422 and one problem type.
     - AC: SYS-R31, ACC-AC16

     4. Low: SYS-AC06 doesn't list the strings that BigInt() silently accepts
     - specs/000-overview/spec.md:171
     - What is wrong: the rejection list has " 1050" and "". It does not have "0x10", "0b11", "0o7", "1050 " (trailing whitespace) or "1_000". BigInt() turns several of these into numbers (BigInt("0x10") is 16n, BigInt("1050 ") is 1050n).
     - How to trigger it: an amount schema that does a loose check and then calls BigInt(value) passes SYS-AC06 as written but accepts hex and trailing-space amounts.
     - Impact: amounts that SYS-R07 forbids are accepted.
     - Suggested fix: add those five strings to SYS-AC06's rejected list.
     - AC: SYS-AC06 / SYS-R07

     5. Low: a test with no assertions counts as proof
     - vitest.config.ts:18-35
     - What is wrong: you blocked fails tests. Empty or no-assertion tests are not blocked.
     - How to trigger it: it('ACC-AC05 rejects bad currencies', async () => { await post(...) }) with no expect passes, and the AC is covered.
     - Impact: an AC looks proven by a test that asserts nothing.
     - Suggested fix: set expect: { requireAssertions: true } in both projects. Add a sample to test/fixtures/forbid-fails.
     - AC: missing AC (tooling)

     6. Low: a wrongly named tasks file is silently ignored
     - scripts/traceability.ts:270-285
     - What is wrong: the loader reads only the exact name tasks.md. It ignores Tasks.md or task.md, and any tasks file in a folder without spec.md. The loader explicitly guards against Spec.md this way, but not against these.
     - How to trigger it: write specs/003-movements/Tasks.md and tick tasks in it. The gate enforces none of them and reports no problem.
     - Impact: ticked tasks lose enforcement without any warning.
     - Suggested fix: report any .md file in a spec folder other than spec.md, plan.md or tasks.md, and any tasks file that has no spec.md next to it.
     - AC: missing AC (tooling)

     7. Low: a Verified by line passes whatever it says
     - scripts/traceability.ts:389
     - What is wrong: for a level-ci AC, any text after Verified by: makes it covered. Nothing checks that the named command or CI job exists.
     - How to trigger it: - **Verified by:** CI job terraform on an Implemented spec while ci.yml has no such job. The gate passes.
     - Impact: small today, because no real spec has a ci AC yet.
     - Suggested fix: require the value to name a job in .github/workflows/ci.yml and check that the job exists.
     - AC: missing AC (tooling)

     8. Low: the docs differ slightly from what the gate does
     - specs/README.md:22-24: the status table says Draft and Approved ACs without proof are pending, and that Implemented ACs without proof always fail. In fact, a ticked task makes a Draft AC fail. An Implemented e2e AC with no report shows as unverified and passes without --require.
     - specs/README.md:58: says an AC heading must be followed by a space. The pattern at traceability.ts:17 also accepts a bare ### MOV-AC03 with nothing after it.
     - specs/001-accounts/spec.md:6: "Depends on specs" leaves out the ledger spec (002) and the movements spec (003), although ACC-R19, ACC-AC16 and ACC-AC17 rely on them.
     - .claude/skills/spec/template.md:48: the open-questions table has no # column, but both specs number their questions (Q1, Q2, ...) and cite them.
     - Impact: confusion only.
     - Suggested fix: align the text with the gate's behaviour.
     - AC: none

     Deferred, already planned: specs 002 to 007 (ledger, movements, idempotency, auth, runtime), which specs 000 and 001 point to and the phase still has to write; ADRs (03-adrs); plan.md and tasks.md (04-plans); the e2e project, the e2e suite in CI and --require e2e (phases 11 and 12); the freshness check for docs/traceability.md (final phase); the 02-specs transcript and its row in docs/ai/README.md (when /ship closes the phase); CURSOR_SECRET in .env.example; JWT issuer and audience rules (spec 006); timeout ordering and pool sizing (spec 007).

     - Transactions and concurrency: no findings. The specs require ordered locks, checks after locks, bounded retries and no in-process state.
     - Idempotency: no findings. It is deferred to spec 005, and the separate 409 and 503 conditions are already in the error catalogue.
     - Security: no findings beyond #3.
     - Failure handling: no findings. It is deferred to spec 007.

     Follow-ups: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8
     Verdict: ready to ship

      56  - `Level` is exactly `unit`, `integration`, `e2e` or `ci`.

         -FX-ACnn` followed by a space (for example `### MOV-AC3`, `#### 
         -MOV-AC03` or `### MOV-AC03:`) fails the check, and so does an A
         -C ID whose prefix is not the spec's `ID prefix`.

      70  
      71  - An AC is covered only by a test that **passed**, whose full n
          ame (its `describe` titles plus its own title) contains the AC 
          ID, in the report of the project that matches the AC's `Level`.
           A unit test never proves an integration AC.
      72  - Skipped, todo and failed tests prove nothing, and neither doe
          s a test that never ran. A table-driven test counts through eac
          h generated case, for example `it.each(cases)('MOV-AC07 rejects
           %s', ...)`.

         -sed when its body fails.

         +st would report it as passed when its body fails; and a test th
         +at makes no assertion fails (`expect.requireAssertions`).

⏺ I applied the four follow-ups. npm run check passes (29 unit tests), npm run 
  test:integration passes (3 tests), and npm run trace -- --require 
  unit,integration exits 0. It reports 51 ACs: 49 pending and 2 e2e ACs
  unverified. Nothing is committed.

  1. Transfer destinations (finding 3)
  - SYS-R31's 404 step now covers only the account the caller acts on, and
    SYS-R05 makes clear that "transfers out of" means the source account.
  - New SYS-R41: a destination that doesn't exist, is a system account, or is
    another customer's frozen or closed account is refused with one 422. The
    problem type is defined in spec 003, and the bodies differ only in
    requestId.
  - New SYS-AC27 proves it with the four cases, and the error catalogue has a
    row for it.
  - In spec 001, ACC-AC16 and ACC-AC17 now point to SYS-R41. I marked Q8 decided
    by you, since your instruction answers it. Say if you'd rather keep it open
    for the joint review.

  2. Amount strings (finding 4): SYS-AC06 now also rejects "1050 ", "0x10",
  "0b11", "0o7" and "1_000".

  3. Tests without assertions (finding 5)
  - New vitest.shared.ts holds the two test guards: the forbid-fails setup file
    and expect.requireAssertions.
  - Both Vitest projects and the fixture config use it, so the fixture test
    checks the real settings.
  - I renamed the fixture and its test to test-guards. The samples add a sync
    and an async test without assertions, both expected to fail, and the
    ordinary sample now asserts something.
  - All existing unit and integration tests already make assertions, so nothing
    else needed changing.

  4. Docs (finding 8)
  - specs/README.md: the status table now explains ticked tasks on Draft and
    Approved specs, and unverified on Implemented ones. The heading rule allows
    "a space or the end of the line", and the README mentions both test guards.
  - Spec 001's Depends on line now lists 002-ledger and 003-movements. The
    folder names are my guess, since those specs don't exist yet.
  - The spec template's open-questions table has a # column.

  Findings 1, 2, 6 and 7 are left as you decided.
